## Embarcadero golden-hour candidate · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- The independent demo now uses generated façade, road, paving, palm and shoreline textures; a credited CC0 sunset sky; revised lighting, spatial waterfront and bridge detail; and the credited 2020 Roadster with fictional nozzles. The original multiplayer world and simulation are unchanged. Three paired fixed-camera views and all asset credits are linked in [EMBARCADERO_DEMO.md](EMBARCADERO_DEMO.md). The result remains stylized rather than a photographic match.
- Local Chrome 155 / Apple M5 Pro at 1440 × 900 DPR 1: in an ABBA short drive, callback p95 was 9.6–9.7 ms baseline and 9.7–9.9 ms candidate. Candidate run 3 had five intervals over 20 ms, two over 50 ms; the other three driving runs had none. Two followed long CPU render submissions, but the cause is unconfirmed. GPU sampled-render p95 was 3.68–3.74 ms baseline and 3.66–4.80 ms candidate. A cache-disabled local Vite load transferred 3,703,419/6,067,493 encoded bytes. Host load, fixed camera counts, path differences, startup measurements and raw data are in the experiment notes; this is not a physical-device, CDN, multiplayer or capacity claim.
- The local desktop self-drive completed the route, and desktop flight and landing worked. Emulated portrait and landscape touch registered drive and lift at DPR 1; no physical phone was tested. The console reported no errors. `pnpm test` passed 1,348 tests and `pnpm build` passed; both build targets retained existing large-chunk warnings. The 0.0.50 release metadata was added after the measured scene run. The first metadata build failed because `package.json` still said 0.0.49; it was updated to match before the final build.

## Standalone Embarcadero visual experiment · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- The native Cloudflare production build for merge `d2e221748443ded02ca3245cdba8a5f321de043b` and the main no-load checks succeeded. The public demo and 0.0.49 changelog were served; the Roadster GLB matched SHA-256 `e63040bb6f8f2d556ab20b78cc0acdd7674c9061a4c976868f8c957cde55bc40`, and the demo's fingerprinted JavaScript used an immutable cache header. These are not physical-device, sustained-driving or performance checks. Production health reported protocol 57 and configured admission 64, not a measured playable capacity.
- The [hosted paired game observation](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39368959971) compared `227c2fc` with `7c7b00b`: five two-delivery, jump and flight route legs aligned within 0.224 m p95 and 0.28 m maximum. Browser callback p95 was 116.7 / 116.7 ms and room simulated/wall ratio was 0.873 / 0.875 for base / candidate. One-minute load ranged 13.14–15.93 on the hosted three-CPU Mac. This loaded observation is not a performance pass, display or GPU-completion measurement, or an estimate of the separate demo's frame cost.
- The offline prototype adds drive, flight and a self-drive route, a side-by-side live concept view, a licensed 2020 Roadster model with fictional nozzles, and a simplified Embarcadero scene. The remaining differences from the concept are documented in [EMBARCADERO_DEMO.md](EMBARCADERO_DEMO.md).
- Sequential local Chrome 155 traces at 1440 × 900 DPR 1 on Apple M5 Pro used the same short drive/turn/flight/brake inputs, but the resulting trajectories diverged during flight and ended about 16 m apart. The camera also changed. Frame-interval median/p95 was 33.3/34.2 ms before and 33.3/34.1 ms after; median draw calls were 481/547 and triangles 867,514/903,466. These counts are observations from different views, not a controlled estimate of the new scene's rendering cost. One-minute host load was 5.86/5.10, and a separate user demo tab was open. Both traces ran at roughly 30 callbacks per second for an unconfirmed reason. The two runs had no console or page errors. This is one short local callback sample, not GPU completion, a physical device, or a multiplayer benchmark; it does not establish a speedup or rule out a regression. [Raw traces and images](benchmarks/2026-10-19-embarcadero/) are retained.
- `pnpm test` passed 1,346 tests and `pnpm build` passed with the existing large-chunk warnings. The separately built main-game entry was 412,070 Node-gzip bytes, versus 412,071 in the preceding fidelity-lab build; these are entry files, not full startup transfers. A local production-bundle browser check observed self-drive and the side-by-side view. Chrome touch-emulated portrait (390 × 844) and landscape (844 × 390) opened and closed the comparison and accepted a drive-button tap without page or console errors; this is not physical-device or sustained-driving evidence.

## San Francisco fidelity lab · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- The opt-in `?visual=sf-hi` presentation adds generated facade images to 17 ordinary SF building envelopes, a procedural road texture and a Roadster-inspired mesh. It changes no shared map, authoritative physics or protocol. The images are generic AI-generated art, not accurate building photographs; the separate concept gallery is not gameplay. The existing scene and gameplay remain the default.
- One local, sequential Chrome headless comparison on an Apple M5 Pro at 1280 × 720 DPR 1 used the same room code, spawn and scripted W input. At spawn, current/lab frame p95 was 16.8/16.7 ms and draw calls were 286/306; after 3.5 seconds of input, p95 was 16.8/16.8 ms and draw calls were 149/173. Host one-minute load ranged approximately 6–7.5. Speeds and paths differed; 240-frame callback samples are not GPU completion, physical-device, driving-continuity or room-throughput evidence. The existing performance gates remain open. [Raw manifests, trajectories, scenario and screenshots](benchmarks/2026-10-19-sf-fidelity-lab/) are retained.
- Local portrait (390 × 844) and landscape (844 × 390) Chrome touch emulation drove in a disposable sandbox with no browser errors. Desktop exercised driving, turning, the flight input, cockpit view, and the menu switch back to ordinary visuals. These are functional checks, not physical-phone or motion-continuity evidence. The initial portrait runner timeout was an incorrect visibility assertion on the zero-size `body`; the error reported `data-sf-fidelity="ready:17:12"`. A visible lab-label assertion succeeded on retry.
- `pnpm test` passed 1,342 tests, including checks for shared distant Roadster geometry, wheels, nozzles and underbody fans, and the final `pnpm build` passed with its existing large-chunk warning and TypeScript checks. The optional module was 6,728 bytes (3,258 gzip bytes) in the measured build; the two requested WebP textures total 224,050 bytes before HTTP headers. The final entry JavaScript was 412,071 Node-gzip bytes versus 410,668 for the earlier 0.0.47 live entry, a 1,403-byte increase; these are asset sizes, not complete startup transfers. The optional sizes exclude the separately viewed concept gallery and other network traffic. The research and asset pipeline are documented in [SF_FIDELITY_LAB.md](SF_FIDELITY_LAB.md).

## Charger shortcut · protocol v57 candidate · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- Triple Shift and the Actions button request an authoritative route to the lowest-cost currently open, reachable charging bay in the selected self-driving mode. The request does not relocate the player; manual input cancels it. Arrival is reported only after the selected station's refill condition is met inside the target bay.
- Shared-simulation checks cover all four drive modes, refill before arrival, an already-parked car, occupied bays, invalid and airborne requests, and manual takeover. From a South Bay starting point without traffic, all four modes reached the Palo Alto Stanford bay after 119–190 simulated seconds and began refilling; none needed to fly in that fixture. These are sampled routes, not proof of every location, obstacle or flight path.
- On the v57 candidate, all 1,341 unit tests and the build passed; local Wrangler two-client room and prediction integrations passed, including charger request, replication, and manual takeover. Desktop Chrome accepted the Actions button and triple Shift; portrait and landscape layouts were inspected under touch emulation, not on a physical device.
- A local 18-request routing sample at six positions on an active Apple M5 Pro host (one-minute load about 8) took 2.2–35.2 ms for warmed requests and 48.6 ms for the first request, including initial graph setup. The Worker prepares its navigation graph at room startup. These are local function timings, not Worker throughput or room-capacity results.

## Recurring queues and East Bay lanes · protocol v56 candidate · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- New rooms seed six slow cars across three same-direction lanes at the East Bay straight, in each direction, using the existing population budget. In one no-player 600-second shared-simulation run all twelve cars remained undamaged and moving after merging back to the main lane. A second 125-second run with a player elsewhere confirmed the East Bay queues return near their anchors. The queues are short, move, and disperse; this is not continuous congestion everywhere.
- At most one cohort is considered per five simulated seconds. Refresh requires all participating cars to be unattended and both the old and new positions beyond 1,600 m from every player; blocked placements are skipped. Tests cover visibility at either end, two players, a blocked placement, ownership and refresh without adding cars. A San Francisco multi-lane experiment formed a persistent bridge queue and was excluded.
- In a local desktop Chrome sandbox, the East Bay three-lane crawl was visible after teleporting behind it. Portrait and landscape Chrome device-size views displayed their controls, including touch-emulated landscape. These are not physical-device tests or sustained driving proof.
- Two alternating pairs of 1,200 shared steps after warm-up with one idle player measured p95 step time of 1.39–1.73 ms on base and 1.42–1.57 ms on candidate in SF; East Bay was 1.50–1.53 ms on base and 1.42–1.43 ms on candidate. One-minute host load was 8.8–9.2. [Raw samples](benchmarks/2026-10-19-recurring-traffic/samples.json.gz) are retained; this active-host local CPU sample does not establish room throughput, rendered motion or capacity. The prior traffic performance comparison remains HOLD.

## Jump scheduling · protocol v54 candidate · 2026-10-19 Pacific

- The [original desktop failure](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39220974513), [repeat desktop failure](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39267841241), and [touch-emulated portrait failure](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39269042608) remain recorded. These were overloaded hosted runners, not physical-device observations. The older traces do not establish the exact server receipt and timeout timings for every failure.
- A local, two-client Wrangler fixture accepts a jump seven ticks ahead and deliberately skips four tick callbacks while valid controls continue. On base `28e22188`, the accepted edge expired at tick 2, 308 ms after server receipt; with the candidate it was processed exactly once at tick 7 after 587 ms. Raw [base](benchmarks/2026-10-19-delayed-jump/baseline.json.gz) and [candidate](benchmarks/2026-10-19-delayed-jump/candidate.json.gz) traces retain the scheduler receipts, skipped ticks and outcomes. This reproduces the mismatch between the 300 ms edge expiry and the allowed eight-tick window; it does not prove that every historical missed jump had the same cause.
- In the candidate fixture a second press with no continued controls expired without replay, edge outcomes stayed private to its driver, and a reconnected session had no pending press. Scheduler tests also cover jump and flip with active controls, the absolute 700 ms cap, stale revisions, action fences, relocation, duplicate counters and prediction reconstruction. `pnpm test` passed 1,328 tests and `pnpm build` passed with the existing large-chunk warning. The local protocol-v54 prediction and two-client room integrations passed. Further visual and release verification are recorded separately when complete.
- The [hosted visual check](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39276348799) failed overall because the unchanged base again expired its first desktop jump. Candidate `ce4bb43f` processed the first jump on desktop and touch-emulated portrait and landscape, with zero page or console errors. One-minute host load dropped during the run, from 34.98–26.20 on base desktop to 18.66–16.02 on candidate desktop (three virtual CPUs). These are individual Chromium captures, not physical-device, motion-performance or causal-comparison evidence.
- A separate [local CPU sample](benchmarks/2026-10-19-traffic-profile/) on the exact prior release compared one idle-player simulation with 59 seeded jam cars and the same setup with jams removed. It identified `advance` and `trafficInput` as functions to investigate. The Mac was active, the two traces include startup, and this is not a paired room, rendered-motion or production-capacity measurement. The exact-release room comparison remains HOLD.

## Experimental tab capture · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- The [follow-up hosted run](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39214648319) on `69c0cf5` passed the test suite, build, and disposable-game recorder integration, including both signal shutdown cases. Inspection found 270 / 96 video packet gaps of at least 100 ms and one non-increasing candidate packet timestamp. Its quality gate failed; player fidelity remains UNVERIFIED. Both videos and traces were 1280 × 720. One-minute host load at the start and end of the recorded legs was 5.31 → 22.99 / 19.40 → 6.65 on a shared virtual runner. These packet and load observations do not identify the cause or establish the smoothness players see.
- The recorder checks an ephemeral Capture Handle on the selected track, rejects a different target origin, bounds browser-side chunk draining and host-side waits, and keeps uncertain partial files out of CI uploads. It removes failed partial tab recordings when possible. Trace inspection requires a matching handle report. Hosted unit tests and a two-segment disposable-game integration passed, but the later [shutdown integration](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39213004255) failed while a tab stop was pending. Playwright also installs signal handlers that can close the browser independently; the follow-up disables those handlers for interactive sessions so the recorder can drain its queue first. The [next hosted run](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39213753538) passed the disposable-game integration with both SIGINT and SIGTERM while a tab stop was pending; it was canceled before the benchmark completed. Capture Handle does not prove crop, captured-frame cadence or player fidelity; delayed source-change events and in-flight file I/O remain limitations. The retained synthetic videos below remain private and HOLD.
- A short synthetic moving-counter calibration on recorder `e0354a4` used four fresh headed Chrome 155 runs in off/on/on/off order on an Apple M5 Pro (18 logical CPUs, Metal). Animation callback p95 was 17.5 / 17.5 / 17.6 / 17.4 ms; one-minute host load at start / finish was 12.277 → 11.866 / 11.866 → 12.212 / 12.035 → 12.336 / 13.109 → 11.664. This was a synthetic page, not gameplay or display timing. Although the screenshots and requested viewport were 1280 × 720, the two tab tracks reported 2564 × 1390 and 3024 × 1964, respectively. Each trace's byte count matched its video, but dimensions differed and `displaySurface: browser` does not establish which tab was captured. Both recordings are HOLD and remain private until their pixels are inspected. The recorder was copied byte-for-byte into a scratch harness with dependencies from a matching lockfile; installed dependency integrity was not independently verified. No conclusion about player-visible smoothness, capture loss, or the cause of the mismatch is supported.
- The retained [tab-video run on `66eb47e`](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39200717045) had 504 / 343 encoded packet gaps of at least 100 ms (base / candidate). Its candidate video had two non-increasing packet timestamps; its byte count and 1280 × 720 dimensions matched the capture trace. The earlier combined reason did not distinguish these conditions. - A candidate-first [local Metal pair](benchmarks/2026-10-18-hifi-metal/) of those same revisions measured callback p95 16.7/16.8 ms and room simulated/wall 0.933/0.947 for base/candidate, on Apple M5 Pro with 18 logical CPUs. One-minute load was 11.71→21.70 for base and 7.52→11.38 for candidate. Both room ratios were below 0.95; the three route legs aligned under the harness tolerance but the last differed by up to 6.49 m. Tracked files were clean; each report's dirty flag reflects only an untracked dependency symlink. This single, unevenly loaded one-delivery-and-flight comparison does not establish GPU completion, displayed smoothness, a causal improvement or physical-device behavior. It does not erase the hosted signal; a controlled repeat and the two-delivery video remain open.

## Flight balance candidate · protocol v43 · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- Against main `dad912e`, the fixed-tick [flight matrix and motion traces](benchmarks/2026-10-18-flight-balance/) record stock and nozzle levels 1–5 for the Roadster and a rocket-retrofitted Semi. ## Earnings lease timing · 0.0.24 candidate / protocol v43 · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- Fresh disposable sockets reproduced intermittent unavailable earnings status on production and an isolated Cloudflare preview. They sent no identity, controls, purchases or delivery actions. In the 14-second diagnostic preview sample, the first status was valid and six subsequent refreshes were unavailable, while 127 snapshots advanced through tick 252. This does not establish that historical payout settlement was blocked.
- Preview `26370c2` added a rate-limited server log. The captured validation failure had room observation `1792354342268`, post-RPC room time `1792354342319`, lease issue time `1792354342347`, and expiry `1792354357347`: the room rejected a lease issued 28 ms ahead of its clock. Both RPCs completed. [Cloudflare documents production timer values advancing on I/O](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/performance/); the log proves the timestamp gap, not its platform-level cause.
- The fix waits asynchronously only when the issue time fits within the existing five-second observation budget, then rechecks the pending-win/generation barriers and exact lease validity. It does not shift event times, extend stale leases, assume 1× pay, or change physics, snapshots or room capacity.
- The isolated real-room earnings fixture passed a 500 ms ahead lease with continued snapshots, invalidation during that wait, expired and excessively future leases, and recovery to a valid rate. Its full base pickup, 3× delivery, lost-reply, exactly-once income and watchdog-restart flow passed. Two earlier attempts reached the existing delivery receipt but timed out waiting for an injected lost reply: the fixture watched the obsolete `progress.earnings` path. It now intercepts the actual `delivery` transaction and retains its resolved history for receipt replay.
- Before integrating the independent headless-playtest and blog releases, all 876 unit tests, the local build and the standard two-client Wrangler room integration passed. The same 14-second hosted socket probe on fixed preview `eedee91` received seven valid statuses and no unavailable status, with 125 snapshots advancing through tick 248. This is a short functional before/after check, not a latency or capacity benchmark. After integrating independent releases #87 and #88 at `af9ca88`, all 879 unit tests and the build passed; runtime earnings code was unchanged by that merge. Production verification belongs to PR #89; no production delivery or persistent identity was used for diagnosis.

## Local headless playtest recorder · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- After rebasing onto `5c3779d` (protocol v43), 876 tests and `pnpm build` passed. The local recorder integration then exercised a fresh guest browser against isolated Wrangler: unauthorized access was rejected, a held key moved the car, a screenshot and video were saved, and the generated action file replayed successfully in a second room. The test used Metal on a busy Mac; it is not a device-performance benchmark.
- The integration also checked that a pause between control requests becomes a replay wait, including a hold through finish, a zero timeout is rejected, and an empty numeric checkpoint fails. Against a separate local touch-event fixture, Chromium reported that releasing either of two touch IDs left the other active, then reported no active touches after both were released. A fresh emulated portrait game run captured screenshots before and after releasing its controls. These checks are not physical-device evidence.
- On that build, the local staging fixture waited for the requested position to clear, and the received pose revision confirmed `(-180, 0)`, yaw `1.2`, before the 1280 × 720 recording. The video was VP9 WebM, 366 encoded frames at nominal 60 fps over 6.1 seconds; the manifest contained 78 authoritative pose samples and no page or console errors. An extracted frame showed the canvas and HUD. Separate emulated portrait and landscape runs held throttle and steering simultaneously; screenshots showed both controls pressed. These are not physical-device checks.
- On local Wrangler, Playwright 1.64.1 / Chromium 156 on an Apple M5 Pro reported ANGLE Metal. The same driving scenario (SHA-256 `982bb81015508d5ec7bf72ce7aa3f5bd919b8718c610a155d3e2d355115ae326`) produced playable 1280 × 720, nominal 60 fps WebM with live keyboard-driven movement against separately built `82c37f1` and `0aa7bcc`. The frame at two seconds showed the game canvas and instruments. Different room state and traffic mean this demonstrates repeatable inputs and video capture, not an identical route or a version-performance comparison.
- The local staging fixture confirmed its pose revision in received room snapshots before recording. One paired 60 fps video / no-video run used the same scenario and requested start; snapshot positions diverged slightly, as expected from collisions and live simulation. The opt-in trajectory records the controlled driver's authoritative pose only, not display positions or raw player data. Both runs were conducted on a busy shared host (load average approximately 28–36), so no recording-overhead or frame-budget conclusion is supported.
- Emulated 390 × 844 portrait and 844 × 390 landscape runs held and released the on-screen drive control; both HUDs showed movement and produced playable 30 fps recordings. These are Chromium emulations, not physical-device evidence. The loopback control API rejected an invalid secret (401), then accepted authenticated keyboard input, returned a rendered frame and finalized a recording for one disposable browser session.
- A desktop recording's `recordStop` took 35 seconds under host contention while simulation continued; later screenshots therefore did not show the final video moment. Example scenarios now take endpoint screenshots before stopping. Video frame rate is an encoding rate, not proof of distinct game frames, display cadence or GPU completion. No remote VM, Cloudflare container, human remote piloting, or public identity-bound agent API was tested.

An initial full test run exposed a progression test expecting a zero-fare delivery; its expectation was updated for the new minimum before the passing run. The room advertised capacity 8; these checks are not a load test or production verification.

## Integrated service areas and delivery search · local v44 candidate · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- On this local candidate `pnpm test` passed 928 tests and `pnpm build` passed both TypeScript checks, with Vite's existing large-chunk warning. A real local Durable Object journal fixture stopped Wrangler after a non-final assignment checkpoint, restarted it, and settled two already staged delivery stops once at their original event-time rates. The real scheduler selected the UCSF stop after two seeded completions; its delivery and research award each survived a lost reply exactly once. - A synthetic Fremont no-route case used the same ten pickups and ten disconnected ground destinations on both implementations, with navigation graph setup measured separately. Before the Oakland graph change, the prior selector used 513 route plans and took 118.0 ms wall cold and 0.303 ms warm. On the final candidate the incremental selector used 13 plans across 15 batches, taking 12.57 ms wall and 23.51 ms process CPU cold in total, with a 5.62 ms maximum observed batch; an identical warm search took 0.229 ms wall. Graph preparation separately took 60.43 ms wall and 96.69 ms process CPU. Host load and the small graph change confound this comparison; these single samples are not frame-time budgets, physical-device measurements or latency guarantees. Candidate enumeration is limited to 512 pair scans and two route plans per batch; graph initialization and fixed-map index work have separate costs.
- The standard two-client Wrangler room integration passed on the final routing candidate at protocol v44 and advertised capacity eight; it is not a capacity or ferry-crossing test. An earlier attempt timed out after a source edit reloaded Wrangler during the run. Chrome displayed the actual local multiplayer game on desktop and in emulated portrait and landscape: a touch-emulated drive moved the car, the map opened, and selecting locked Oakland showed the locked-area message. The final candidate's small-screen HUD remains crowded. No physical device or production deployment was checked.



## Provisional 64-player admission candidate · 2026-10-18 Pacific

- Based on `82c37f1` / protocol v42, the capacity candidate passed all 848 tests and `pnpm build`; the existing Vite large-chunk warning remains. A local Wrangler instance using the checked-in configuration advertised capacity 64. With a separate capacity-2 Wrangler instance, the room-capacity integration verified a 4003 full-room close, a separate named room and reuse of a freed seat.
- The first standard two-client room integration reached five Dome goals but timed out waiting for the winning-player and both Roadster award assertions. Its cause has not been established. After merging the radio release into candidate `67cbd1c`, a separate rerun of the full local Wrangler integration passed, including the match winner, awards, movement and disconnect checks; it advertised capacity 64. This is a functional check, not a load test. The previous v32 64-client local throughput sample and its shared-host limits are recorded below. No new sustained 64-player throughput, browser or physical-device result is claimed here.
- On protocol v43 candidate `fb87ecb`, all 876 tests, the build, the standard two-client room integration, and the capacity-2 full-room and freed-seat integration passed. The dedicated branch Preview advertised 64 and served the same entry JavaScript as the local build. The 65th connection in the bounded preview sample received code 4003. These checks establish admission behavior, not playable throughput.
- Sequential 60-second preview samples using the simpler `RoomInputClient` saw all 8 or 64 participants remain connected and advance at least 60.2 simulated seconds. At 64 the helper recorded 47,542 rejected inputs including warmup, a 300 ms p95 snapshot gap, and 5,127 MiB of decoded JSON across all clients. At 8, it recorded 607 rejections including warmup, a 164 ms p95 gap, and 164 MiB of decoded JSON. Rejection totals include warmup, whereas the sent totals in the raw files describe the measurement window. Each sample used one load-generator host and a different input scheduler from the game; host load ranged 35–62 on 18 logical CPUs across the runs. See the [8-client](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/preview-8.json) and [64-client](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/preview-64.json) traces.
- A separate 20-second synthetic test using real `Session` and `MotionClock` instances, without browser rendering, observed 19.8 seconds of game time and 24 late rejections at 8 clients; at 64 it observed only 13.6 seconds minimum / 14.7 median and captured 9,887 late rejections for measurement-window sends. One second of response draining means these rejections are incomplete. The 64-client test decoded 1,256 MiB on one host, with 339 ms p95 snapshot gaps and client event-loop delay of 45.4 ms p95. The host's one-minute load was 20.7–25.2 on 18 logical CPUs, compared with 33.1–40.0 for the 8-client run. The [8-client trace](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/preview-session-8.json.gz) and [64-client trace](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/preview-session-64.json.gz) do not isolate server, network, or generator bottlenecks. A subsequent client change keeps unchanged rejected controls on the normal heartbeat while preserving prompt changed controls and button presses; it requires a new capacity comparison.
- On retry-fix candidate `176bbbc`, all 877 tests, the build, and local Wrangler room and prediction integrations passed. A repeat 64-client Session sample was severely contended: during its 20.2-second measurement host load rose from about 28 to 80 on 18 logical CPUs and client event-loop delay was 361 ms p95. Clients observed 1.2–1.4 simulated seconds, and the last snapshots showed applied input for 12 of 64 clients. The local room integration overlapped part of the run. This [repeat trace](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/preview-session-64-after.json.gz) does not establish whether the retry change improves capacity; the one-second response drain was also incomplete.
- On dedicated Preview build `21577e2`, four separate GitHub-hosted runners each drove 16 real `Session` instances in one synchronized room for approximately 20 seconds. All 64 stayed connected, saw all 64 players, showed applied input, and observed at least 19.9 seconds of simulated time. Of 25,612 measurement-window inputs, 177 late rejections were captured; the one-second drain could miss delayed replies. Per-runner p95 snapshot gaps were 124–134 ms and event-loop delays 21–25 ms. The runners decoded 1,704 MiB of JSON in total, and their sampled one-minute loads ranged 0.17–0.69 on four logical CPUs each. [Four raw traces and the harness](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/) preserve the room epoch, source hashes and runner timings. The distributed result identifies single-host contention as a substantial confounder in the prior samples, but does not isolate the retry change, render a browser frame, or establish sustained or physical-device performance. The diagnostic workflow is archived with the harness and is not installed as a permanent Actions job.
- After integrating earnings fix `aeb5704`, Preview build `016e1ee` ran the same four-runner, 64-Session setup for 60 seconds after warm-up. All 64 clients remained online, saw all players and showed applied input; each observed at least 60.0 simulated seconds, meeting the 57-second simulation-progress target. Of 76,814 measured sends, 559 late rejections were captured; the one-second drain may miss delayed responses. Per-runner p95 snapshot gaps were 120–125 ms (maximum 694 ms), rejection-response p95 was 161–414 ms, and event-loop p95 was 21–25 ms. The four hosts each had four logical CPUs and sampled one-minute loads of 0.10–0.50; they decoded 5,115 MiB of JSON in aggregate, not measured wire bytes. The harness aborts if a client send buffer exceeds 1 MB. Server tick work, server queue/history bounds, successful-input acknowledgement latency, and wire bytes were not measured, so the remaining throughput targets and playable capacity are not established. The [raw traces and harness](benchmarks/2026-10-18-capacity-preview/) record the common room epoch, source hashes and timing; this was a preview sample without browser rendering or physical devices.
- On `016e1ee`, `pnpm test` passed 880 tests and `pnpm build` passed with the existing large-chunk warning. Local Wrangler room and prediction integrations passed with protocol v43 and capacity 64; a separate capacity-2 fixture again passed full-room rejection, a distinct room and freed-seat reuse.
- After incorporating the live economy and mobile HUD releases into `a3297d6`, all 894 tests and the build passed; the existing Vite large-chunk warning remains. Local Wrangler room and prediction integrations passed at protocol v43/capacity 64, and the separate capacity-2 fixture passed full-room rejection and freed-seat reuse. The only shared simulation change since the 60-second preview sample was the constant-time delivery payout floor; the sample remains attributed to `016e1ee`.

- Focused simulation checks cover smoother steering through a city route, gradual following behind a stopped vehicle, resumption after it clears, emergency braking and route completion. These are specific arcade scenarios rather than universal safety or production performance claims.
- Local Chrome desktop double-tapped Shift and began a delivery route. In emulated 390×844 portrait, a touch on the delivery shortcut started the route and the car drove autonomously; the Scores and Awards panels were inspected in portrait and 844×390 landscape. Their scrollable headers kept the close control visible. No physical device was tested. The local client prediction integration also passed.
- A held guest-save binding across a Rocket Dome finish retained the award exactly once in the original wallet, including after restart. A different incoming wallet did not inherit the old driver's result or in-progress game evidence. The fixture supplied prior touches and positioned the ball; normal game physics scored the final goal. If a different-driver recovery acquires its profile while the old driver earns an award, that recovery can be rejected after an existing session for the incoming driver was disconnected; its wallet remains recoverable. An atomic, reversible handoff is not covered here.

Of 649 samples, 541 included the room tick in their stack: approximately 541 ms at the requested interval, not exact tick CPU because of sampling, scheduling and GC attribution. Callback wall p50/p95 was 2/3 and 3/8 versus 1/2 ms straight, and 3/6 and 3/4 versus 1/2 ms turning. All 17 common fixed-tick turn checkpoints matched at reported precision; no inputs were rejected. Different host load and randomized candidate rosters confound causal attribution. An earlier run with 16 cabs had a noisy 19.58 ms p95 step versus 4.34 ms without cabs. On the main baseline, 64 clients saw all peers with no disconnects over 10 seconds, but the room advanced only 4.9 simulated seconds and delivered 5.05 snapshots per client per second (p95 gap 283 ms). The clients parsed 452.83 MiB of decoded JSON; this is not measured wire traffic. The load-generator event-loop p95 was 24.3 ms. These are evidence of slowdown on that same host, not a safe room limit or a production-capacity measurement.
- An experimental common-snapshot serialization approach reduced isolated construction CPU in a synthetic batch, but a later 64-client run advanced only 0.6–0.7 simulated seconds in 10.31 wall seconds, with client event-loop p95 168.3 ms and heavy concurrent system load. Different host contention prevents attributing the discrepancy to the change. The experiment was removed pending representative Worker/send-path and network measurements; there is no validated capacity improvement here.

- `pnpm test` passed 62 tests; `pnpm build` passed both TypeScript checks and the Vite build. Vite still warns that the main JavaScript chunk exceeds 500 kB (about 635 kB / 174 kB gzip). The source extract, generated OSM-derived trace and bake script were emitted as downloadable build assets; a clean temporary bake reproduced the checked-in trace.
- The approximately 19.86 km loop was simulated in both directions. Controlled San Jose bend approaches at initial 160 and 220 km/h stayed within 4.1 m of the centerline in both directions; a 259 km/h boosted reverse approach left the pavement. The fictional hill and overpass, including ramp traversals at 100, 160 and 220 km/h and stacked-vehicle collisions, were checked in simulation. They were not manually driven in the browser.
- Chrome desktop on the local build: drove across the Bay Bridge at up to 183 km/h, saw the Oakland approach, switched the local/Bay map, and lifted to 22 m while air decreased. The 06:00-start clock advanced. Local Chrome 390×844 portrait and 844×390 landscape touch emulation showed the driving controls; portrait acceleration moved the car, and the landscape scores dialog opened and closed. Invalid room input showed the validation message, and a browser joined a local multiplayer room. These checks are emulation, not physical-device or frame-rate evidence.
- Local Wrangler two-client integration passed for clock, terrain support, traffic, delivery/police, fleet selection, remote movement, invalid input and disconnect cleanup. A separate eight-second local same-host 64-client smoke passed: all clients saw all players, minimum 69 snapshots/client and p95 snapshot delivery gap 128 ms. It started at dawn with fewer NPCs than the rush target; it is not a WAN, sustained-load or production test.
- A separate local Node probe at the morning peak used 64 players and 43 NPCs after warm-up. Three repetitions measured 4.18–9.40 ms p95 per 50 ms simulation step, with a 115.8 ms maximum in one run. Another run under concurrent host work measured 166 ms p95. These noisy same-host observations are not a Workers performance guarantee.
- The traffic profile, bridge meter and highway incident are fictional arcade scenarios; the bridge meter controls eastbound NPCs and AVs, not manual drivers. Sparse traffic and synthetic queue tests do not prove realistic Bay congestion. I-280, the other Bay crossings and real interchanges are not implemented. No physical-device, sustained peak-traffic, geographic-latency or new deployed verification has been performed.

- Deployed64-client smoke: all connected and saw64 peers; minimum80 snapshots/client; p95 inter-snapshot gap114ms during an8-second measurement. All synthetic clients originated from one machine. The measurement is delivery interval, NOT input latency or end-to-end round-trip time.
- Live `/api/health` returned `{"ok":true,"version":1,"capacity":64}`.

Physical device behavior and sustained FPS are not verified. Roll controls were moved into a clear strip above pedals after layout inspection; portrait camera aim adjusted to keep the car above the instruments.
- Screenshots: `screenshots/live-desktop.jpg`, `screenshots/mobile-portrait.jpg`, `screenshots/mobile-landscape.jpg`.

## Known limits / next checks

No sustained load,128-player load, geographic multi-region latency, adversarial abuse soak or actual phone thermals/frame rates tested. Public room codes offer no privacy guarantee. Active rooms use billed DO duration and WebSocket messages; total public-room creation is not globally quota-limited by this app. Monitor usage before broadly promoting the sandbox.

Multiplayer presentation interpolates server snapshots without client prediction; remote latency can be felt. Camera can clip scenery at awkward angles; collision geometry is simplified. Trees/charger posts/bridge furniture are decorative. At that baseline there was no sound, NPC traffic, quests, persistent inventory, shooting or death/respawn; NPC traffic has since been added. At that baseline, playable streets were flat and hills were scenery; the map is not an accurate road network.

Primary Tesla pages read2026-10-17; site identity/address only, not live stall availability or real air service:

- SF16th Street,2300 16th St: https://www.tesla.com/en_CA/findus/location/supercharger/8739
- SF Geary Boulevard,2675 Geary Blvd: https://www.tesla.com/en_GB/findus/location/supercharger/SanFranciscoCAsupercharger2
- Oakland2nd Street,255 2nd Street: https://www.tesla.com/en_gb/findus/location/supercharger/oaklandcasupercharger
- San Mateo Park Place,1200 Park Place: https://www.tesla.com/en_CA/findus/location/supercharger/sanmateosupercharger

Cloudflare reference: https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/state/ documents a32,768 hibernation-API WebSocket connection ceiling and explicitly notes practical CPU/memory limits. This game uses standard accepted WebSockets and an active physics timer; that ceiling is not its gameplay capacity. Reference: https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/best-practices/websockets/ .

## 2026-10-18 · Automatic multiplayer and room overflow

In the isolated multiplayer worktree, `pnpm test` passed 275 tests and `pnpm build` passed. Local Wrangler with `MAX_PLAYERS=2` passed the protocol-v20 two-client room integration and a separate capacity test: the third connection received close code 4003, an independently named room accepted a player, and a freed seat accepted a replacement. One earlier room integration run timed out at the existing soccer-goal assertion while tests and build were running concurrently; a subsequent isolated run passed.

Chrome automatically entered `bay`, and a third client saw the full-room prompt. Naming a different room connected successfully, and keyboard driving advanced the car. The game and overflow prompt were inspected at desktop, emulated 390×844 portrait and 844×390 landscape. Keyboard activation of the alternate-room button worked; emulated pointer activation was not verified at landscape size. No physical phone was tested.

Short local synthetic load checks with all clients and Wrangler on the same machine are not Cloudflare capacity measurements. With the cached serializer, 64 clients sent 12,608 inputs over 10 seconds, received 6.6 snapshots per client per second and advanced the room 6.5 simulated seconds; aggregate received snapshots were about 600 MiB. A 128-client, 6-second run received 3.07 snapshots per client per second and advanced 1.8 simulated seconds, with about 570 MiB received. There were no observed socket closures during either measurement. These runs show substantial local slowdowns and bandwidth; the 64-player production cap remains provisional. `scripts/load-room.ts` can repeat the experiment against a local server or an authorized preview.

The Cloudflare staging preview advertised protocol v20 and an experimental capacity of 128. Its two-client gameplay integration passed. In separate four-second synthetic tests from one client machine, 64 clients stayed connected, received 10.93 snapshots per client per second, advanced 4.2 simulated seconds and received about 397 MiB in aggregate; the p95 snapshot gap was 154 ms. At 128 clients, all stayed connected, but received 2.45 snapshots per client per second, advanced 1.1 simulated seconds and received about 302 MiB; the p95 snapshot gap was 1,585 ms. Client processing and network conditions also affect these measurements. These short runs are not a soak test, a measurement of input latency or evidence that 64 players is reliably playable.

After integrating main's updated Rocket Island physics and bumping the protocol to v21, `pnpm test` passed 308 tests and `pnpm build` passed. Local Wrangler passed the two-client gameplay integration and the two-seat overflow integration. In Chrome, a newly connected player drove immediately without opening a menu; in a full two-seat room, choosing the same room retained the connection and active delivery time. The earlier Cloudflare load figures describe the prior v20 preview, not this combined v21 build.

The combined v21 Cloudflare branch preview passed the two-client gameplay integration. Short synthetic tests from one client machine again show that connected sockets are not a capacity guarantee: 64 clients received 4.02 snapshots per client per second and observed 1.5 simulated seconds over 4.01 seconds; 128 clients received 0.24 snapshots per client per second over 4.13 seconds, and the sampled player received too few snapshots to measure simulation progress. No sockets closed during the measurement, although the 128-client process needed interruption while closing. A subsequent 32-client run observed 1.6 simulated seconds over four seconds, but may have been affected by residual load from the prior test. These are brief, synthetic observations, not a sustained or isolated capacity benchmark. Production's 64-player cap remains provisional, and performance may degrade well before the room is full.

After incorporating the driving-feel and collision changes from main, protocol v22 passed 316 tests, the build, the local two-client room integration and the two-seat full-room/alternate-room integration. The v21 Cloudflare load results above do not measure this merged v22 build.

After integrating the China Basin Dome and economy updates, protocol v23 passed 337 tests, the build, the local two-client gameplay integration and the two-seat full-room/alternate-room integration. Earlier Cloudflare load results do not measure this v23 build.

While an existing driver rebinds the same wallet, an active OTA period continues using room time even though their controls and progression are paused. Loading a different saved wallet restores its remaining period after acquisition. A stalled recovery can therefore consume part of an already-active subscription.

No physical device was tested.

With the storage correction integrated into the protocol v37 cruise-and-awards candidate, `pnpm test` passed 713 tests and `pnpm build` passed. ## 2026-10-18 · Selective performance observations

Against clean game revision `aeb5704c62edde147ff3441b9f82d35ec3d3a63f` (0.0.24-alpha, protocol v43), the isolated Playwright/Metal drive advanced 296.729 m during the sampled segment. Browser callback interval p95 was 16.8 ms across 360 intervals; the local one-client room progressed 59.2 simulation seconds over 61.912 seconds of observed snapshot arrivals, mostly without held controls after a short drive. The Apple M5 Pro host was active (one-minute load average 9.00 before and 10.09 after); this is not a sustained driving, capacity or physical-device measurement. Deterministic fast-slalom results were 0.983–1.052× displayed/body speed and 8.333/16.667 ms modeled steering onset with steady 150 ms simulated RTT. Bunched delivery yielded 0.898–1.171× and 46 rejected inputs, outside the existing aspirational smoothness range. See [the version log and retained traces](PERFORMANCE_BASELINES.md). The measured game revision predates the economy merge `04f4d16`.

After incorporating that economy merge, `pnpm test` passed 889 tests and `pnpm build` passed with the existing Vite large-chunk warning. A disposable local Wrangler recorder integration passed real driving, video, replay, frame sampling and touch-control checks using Playwright Chromium/Metal. `actionlint` validated the selective workflow locally. Hosted CI and deployment are separate observations.

A second run of the same clean game revision and local workload gave 16.8 ms frame p95 and 59.2 simulated seconds over 61.928 seconds of received snapshots (0.956×), with the same synthetic values. One-minute host load averages were 7.95 before and 10.02 after; the two observations do not establish a performance threshold. The repeat report and raw browser observations are retained alongside the initial trace.

The clean 0.0.25-alpha revision `1a5a0479570f1bde7d33ed6218c0c0df670767d2` on the same M5 Pro reported a 16.7 ms browser p95 across 360 intervals and 0.952 room sim/wall ratio, with 0.983–1.052× synthetic speed and 8.333/16.667 ms modeled steering onset. One-minute load was 20.15 before and 13.66 after on 18 logical CPUs. See the version log for the report and raw evidence. Different reporter hashes mean this is not a paired comparison with 0.0.24.

The merged 0.0.27-alpha revision `00f8ffeeaefa7a184816244c26b515cf19585539` passed 899 tests and the production build. A separate M5 Pro/Metal observation recorded 16.7 ms browser p95 across 360 intervals, 0.983–1.052× synthetic speed, 8.333/16.667 ms modeled steering onset and 0.914 room sim/wall ratio. One-minute host load rose from 16.15 to 34.72 on 18 logical CPUs; this is not a paired regression or capacity result. The version log retains the report and raw traces.

A disposable GitHub-hosted `macos-15` probe ran the 720p scenario with headless Chromium reporting `ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple Paravirtual device`. All four observations were available: 66.7 ms browser p95 across 165 intervals and 0.930 room sim/wall ratio, with the same synthetic metrics; one-minute VM load was 20.42 before and 8.47 after on three virtual CPUs. This is a single VM probe, not a physical-device or GPU-completion measurement. The pickup emblem pixels in the staged desktop crop matched between versions, including a delayed screenshot.
- The earlier [hosted pair](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39171031487) observed callback interval p95 100 → 116.7 ms on a heavily loaded 3-vCPU runner. A later [repeat](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39189816671) compared main `e07f0be` with candidate `44bd3cb` on the same 3-vCPU VM class: callback p95 was 133.3 → 116.7 ms, room simulated/wall ratio 0.925 → 0.931, and sampled route distance p95 / max 0.385 / 0.579 m. One-minute host load fell 13.97 → 9.38 during the base and 8.15 → 6.10 during the candidate. The observations used one delivery, two jumps and powered flight, with video off; animation callback timing is not GPU completion or display presentation. Both pairs are descriptive and neither confirms a regression, improvement or performance gate. The [post-integration pair](https://github.com/swyxio/TeslaRoadster6/actions/runs/39190046667) compared `a3bde95` with `8f7c9ce`: callback p95 was 116.7 / 116.7 ms, room ratio 0.911 / 0.912, and sampled path distance p95 / max 1.179 / 1.621 m. One-minute load fell 10.63 → 4.79 on the base and 4.77 → 2.39 on the candidate; its one-delivery, two-jump and flight scenario also used no video. Its load and callback timing leave the performance gate open. Driving continuity, physical mobile and wider fleet-preview coverage remain open; do not release the upgrade based on these observations alone.
